Zones can produce reversals and they can also be broken. Once you master defining your zones, you will need to analyze the past at the origin of the zone, the approach to the zone, understand the price move and dynamic, and then apply upon price action. The confirmation for the Weekly level to short, was the LTF Can Can / QM price action. I think we both have similar analysis, I care mostly about LTF confirmation of HTF levels. I dont think there is such thing of over trading, your enthusiasm for this profession will win eventually, and you will be benefited with the best job on earth only few can do. My break was when I started trading HTF levels with LTF confirmation. taking only most extreme levels has so many advantages. 1. first your risk is very low, and so the time it take for conviction of the trade success potential. 2. with the extremes, most of the time you have the privileged to profit from minor reactions. so your winning and gain experience is much greater. 3. at the extremes your psychology state is very comfortable. you dont sweat while trading, and you learn to just hate any minor drawdown. Hope it helps a little Keep on the trading enthusiasm. G Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 26 Mar 2014 18:44 #114 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 I got this question in private message, I think it is better to reply it in public as I get similar questions: Hi Benhur, first, a very big thanks to your generous sharing of your charts and insights in the forum - your posts have been a real inspiration and eye-opener to a fledging trader like me. In particular, I think how you approach the market really makes sense - especially the part on LTF confirmation of HTF levels. If you don't mind, I have 2 questions: 1. You mentioned that the levels structure you use most is the QM. Does that mean that you start your analysis by looking for QM levels at significant H4/D1 swing highs or swing lows (which I assume are the extremes)? 2. As for LTF confirmation, are you referring to the LTF structure at the source of the HTF level or LTF entry set-up? Looking at your charts, you based your entry on some LTF entry set-ups when the price returns to the level, but do you also look at the LTF at the source of the HTF level to pinpoint the exact level? Basically I am scanning for QM levels of HTF. If the level itself is at extreme, I would be more comfortable to take it as is. There are more QM levels at Mid way of HTF - those offer scalp opportunities. LTF confirmation would be a newly forming QM structure occurring at logical HTF level and which is also explainable with LTF level at the origin of the HTF level i apply. LTF analysis of the origin provides the necessary resolution for better accuracy. TF are just price wrapped in arbitrary time period, while price is has a definite targets regardless of TF. Therefore MTF analysis is so much important for our work. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. I understand that the goal is to focus on only the most significant levels, trade edges etc for high probability, and I'm making some great trades like this. But sometimes, I see what to all intents and purposes appears to be an important FTR get taken out and a PAZ entered, only for price to then turn from a seemingly random place, and no matter how much I try to apply RTM reasoning and methodology to it, it simply doesn't make sense why it turned from where it did. I then get "stuck" with incorrect directional bias, expecting a PAZ to complete, which never does (although sometimes in fairness I've seen an incomplete PAZ move get completed days or even weeks later). I put this down initially to inexperience, but I'm pretty confident in marking up the correct FLs on the charts now, and 9 times out of 10, even if I get something wrong, I understand why with hindsight. It's the 1 in 10 that's driving me nuts. Much of what I see that confuses me can be explained if you consider the DP to engulf a FL/FTR an important level in its own right, but I was under the impression that it was only FTRs and the engulfs thereof that mattered in the scheme of things (assuming of course the DP isn't an important FL/FTR itself). So two questions really - to clarify, is it only the FTR formed after an engulf, and not the DP to engulf that is important? And if so, why do we so often see big turning points take place from engulf DPs, when its corresponding FTR has been taken out? And also, are there just times you have to shrug your shoulders, and move on? Or is it possible to understand every move? All the best, thanks for any help. Hope you don't mind me polluting your great journal.. :) Please consider the following example. This is a USDCAD chart. I watched IF's decision points video over and over again. He says in the video that, price goes from one decision point to another. In the below H1 chart.. I have marked a DP which decides price is too expensive and then decides to go down the SR flip. IF shows that the decision is made at the open of the candle. (at least that's what I saw in the video). 1. But how do we really say that the decision is made at the open of the candle? when we go to LTFs, candle is narrowed down we can exactly find the LTF candle which broke the level. So the DP will be the opening point of the LTF candle.. Still if we have more and more LTFs.. we can further go down and narrow down. So which DP is the correct DP which decides the price is too expensive and broke the S/R flip level? DP1, DP2 or DP3? 2. Other thing is, If price is reacting to a DP, why would we need FTRs, FLs? In the example, the DP is different from the FTR. Price can react to the FTR or DP. Do we put our pending orders at FTR and Stoplosses on DPs ( in this example)? 3. Are FLs, FTRs new decision points made after the previous decision took place? ( For example, DP1 decided that price is too expensive and broke down the SR flip level and then made the new DP2) Note: Price actually reacted to DP1. never reacted to the FTR.. I dont know why. The following user(s) said Thank You: salivan , JAVAD , ericpurdy Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 26 Sep 2016 11:55 #186 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 Hi VT The moment when things clicked for me in my studies was the moment I realized nothing in the market is technical. Instead I started looking for places where I could find unfilled orders. Today, when I look at the chart I am searching for the ultimate bouncing levels, then I consider if I want to trade these levels. At decision points there are unfilled orders and you can expect a bounce there. but be aware that a decision level is just the nearby barrier of a PAZ (price Action Zone). The further boundary of that PAZ can react too, because this is the first level that price was balanced with opposing orders, therefor just beyond it you can find unfilled orders. This is why you should consider FTR, FL, DP, CP, QM, DM - these all are variety of formations that unfold imbalanced order flow that you can use in your trading. To make things more complex, yes you should drill down forever if possible to really fine your level. You should always remember that time is no factor of the market, therefore time frame is just a random necessity to document price. The events of the markets are documented by price only. So our job is harder to extract the finest level to act upon. Yes is no that straight forward, And yes we should be very happy it is like that, only the busy miners get reach the precious rocks. To be more practical, do what I did for weeks during my initial learning phase; take two lines one for above price one for below market price. stare at one minute charts and place you lines where you know price will give a bounce. Do so for whole sessions with great patience and motivation to really see price in action. If you do so, come back and share with the RTM community your finding. I am sure there are going to be at least few priceless insights you take for life. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 02 Oct 2016 15:46 #192 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 I got this question in my PM, I think it worth to post my reply in public. The question was related to my definition for Flag-Limits. as some of you know, I tend to breakdown everything to the OF arrays, by doing this I can assume where unfilled orders are located: Think of a Flag-Limit in terms of orderflow balance / imbalance. The flag area show us where there was balanced OF, while the pole (the approach or the break) is where we assume to find imbalance. We also assume that we can find unfilled orders where there was first (or last) imbalanced OF. This, in theory explains the FL under and above. The question was When you said while the pole the approach or the break .. Do you mean the last H made inside a Flag before let's say price Engulfs down with LQ ? and by break you mean the Break of Support or Demand in the Flag ? also can't figure out exactly what do you mean by first or last Imbalanced ? Here is an image to explain how FO aligned around FL Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 12 Oct 2016 15:04 #196 ViTaLDRuG --> Offline Trader Posts: 280 Thank you received: 521 Hi Benhur, I noticed that, if a FL is broken, and then if another FL is created which hold the price up or down (It will hold the price up or down opposite to the direction the previous FL hold) and then the second FL is broken, that breaking swing will always go to the decision point there it was decided to break the first FL. Noticed this almost every time. In case of a perfect Flag (which has a big pole up/down), if its FL is broken, the price should go to the start of the pole. Of course the start of that pole should break another FL on the way to create the current Flag' FL. benhur wrote: ViTaLDRuG wrote: Where do I place these two lines? You mentioned that the levels structure you use most is the QM. Does that mean that you start your analysis by looking for QM levels at significant H4/D1 swing highs or swing lows (which I assume are the extremes)? 2. As for LTF confirmation, are you referring to the LTF structure at the source of the HTF level or LTF entry set-up? Looking at your charts, you based your entry on some LTF entry set-ups when the price returns to the level, but do you also look at the LTF at the source of the HTF level to pinpoint the exact level? Basically I am scanning for QM levels of HTF. If the level itself is at extreme, I would be more comfortable to take it as is. There are more QM levels at Mid way of HTF - those offer scalp opportunities. LTF confirmation would be a newly forming QM structure occurring at logical HTF level and which is also explainable with LTF level at the origin of the HTF level i apply. TF are just HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT price wrapped in arbitrary time period, while price is has a definite targets regardless of TF. Therefore MTF analysis is so much important for our work. IF shows that the decision is made at the open of the candle. (at least that's what I saw in the video). https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 10/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 1. But how do we really say that the decision is made at the open of the candle? when we go to LTFs, candle is narrowed down we can exactly find the LTF candle which broke the level. So the DP will be the opening point of the LTF candle.. Still if we have more and more LTFs.. we can further go down and narrow down. So which DP is the correct DP which decides the price is too expensive and broke the S/R flip level? DP1, DP2 or DP3? 2. Other thing is, If price is reacting to a DP, why would we need FTRs, FLs? Are FLs, FTRs new decision points made after the previous decision took place? ( For example, DP1 decided that price is too expensive and broke down the SR flip level and then made the new DP2) Note: Price actually reacted to DP1. never reacted to the FTR.. I dont know why. Thanks in advance. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 11/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUACttAaTchImOeNnts: FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: salivan, JAVAD, ericpurdy QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Benhur Journal #186 benhur Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2231 Hi VT The moment when things clicked for me in my studies was the moment I realized nothing in the market is technical. Instead I started looking for places where I could find unfilled orders. Today, when I look at the chart I am searching for the ultimate bouncing levels, then I consider if I want to trade these levels. At decision points there are unfilled orders and you can expect a bounce there. but be aware that a decision level is just the nearby barrier of a PAZ (price Action Zone). The further boundary of that PAZ can react too, because this is the first level that price was https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 12/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community balanced with opposing orders, therefor just beyond it you can find unfilled orders. This HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT is why you should consider FTR, FL, DP, CP, QM, DM - these all are variety of formations that unfold imbalanced order flow that you can use in your trading. To make things more complex, yes you should drill down forever if possible to really fine your level. You should always remember that time is no factor of the market, therefore time frame is just a random necessity to document price. The events of the markets are documented by price only. So our job is harder to extract the finest level to act upon. Yes is no that straight forward, And yes we should be very happy it is like that, only the busy miners get reach the precious rocks. To be more practical, do what I did for weeks during my initial learning phase; take two lines one for above price one for below market price. stare at one minute charts and place you lines where you know price will give a bounce. Do so for whole sessions with great patience and motivation to really see price in action. If you do so, come back and share with the RTM community your finding. I am sure there are going to be at least few priceless insights you take for life. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 17/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Benhur Journal #192 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT benhur Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2231 I got this question in my PM, I think it worth to post my reply in public. The question was related to my definition for Flag-Limits. as some of you know, I tend to breakdown everything to the OF arrays, by doing this I can assume where unfilled orders are located: Think of a Flag-Limit in terms of orderflow balance / imbalance. The flag area show us where there was balanced OF, while the pole (the approach or the break) is where we assume to find imbalance. We also assume that we can find unfilled orders where there was first (or last) imbalanced OF. This, in theory explains the FL under and above. The question was When you said while the pole the approach or the break .. Do you mean the last H made inside a Flag before let's say price Engulfs down with LQ ? and by break you mean the Break of Support or Demand in the Flag ? also can't figure out exactly what do you mean by first or last Imbalanced ? https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 18/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Here is an image to explain how FO aligned around FL HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: hansma, parnelli, philantrop, Rakkain, althoufx, AranJibril, rick86, symon, Nehru82T, Al3cs, omidfxt, salivan, JAVAD, alirezat https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 19/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Never risk your own capital in trading. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Benhur Journal #196 ViTaLDRuG Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 532 Hi Benhur, I noticed that, if a FL is broken, and then if another FL is created which hold the price up or down (It will hold the price up or down opposite to the direction the previous FL hold) and then the second FL is broken, that breaking swing will always go to the decision point there it was decided to break the first FL. Noticed this almost every time. In case of a perfect Flag (which has a big pole up/down), if its FL is broken, the price should go to the start of the pole. Of course the start of that pole should break another FL on the way to create the current Flag' FL. benhur wrote: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 24/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community ViTHaLODMRuEG wroteE:DWUhCerAeTdIoOINplace thFeOseRtUwMo lines? Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 26 Mar 2014 18:44 #114 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 I got this question in private message, I think it is better to reply it in public as I get similar questions: Hi Benhur, first, a very big thanks to your generous sharing of your charts and insights in the forum - your posts have been a real inspiration and eye-opener to a fledging trader like me. In particular, I think how you approach the market really makes sense - especially the part on LTF confirmation of HTF levels. If you don't mind, I have 2 questions: 1. You mentioned that the levels structure you use most is the QM. Does that mean that you start your analysis by looking for QM levels at significant H4/D1 swing highs or swing lows (which I assume are the extremes)? 2. As for LTF confirmation, are you referring to the LTF structure at the source of the HTF level or LTF entry set-up? Looking at your charts, you based your entry on some LTF entry set-ups when the price returns to the level, but do you also look at the LTF at the source of the HTF level to pinpoint the exact level? Basically I am scanning for QM levels of HTF. If the level itself is at extreme, I would be more comfortable to take it as is. There are more QM levels at Mid way of HTF - those offer scalp opportunities. LTF confirmation would be a newly forming QM structure occurring at logical HTF level and which is also explainable with LTF level at the origin of the HTF level i apply. LTF analysis of the origin provides the necessary resolution for better accuracy. TF are just price wrapped in arbitrary time period, while price is has a definite targets regardless of TF. Therefore MTF analysis is so much important for our work. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 14 Jan 2015 07:13 #171 smimon --> Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Quick question for you pal - am I being overly optimistic in thinking it should be possible to spot the reason for every turn in the market? I understand that the goal is to focus on only the most significant levels, trade edges etc for high probability, and I'm making some great trades like this. But sometimes, I see what to all intents and purposes appears to be an important FTR get taken out and a PAZ entered, only for price to then turn from a seemingly random place, and no matter how much I try to apply RTM reasoning and methodology to it, it simply doesn't make sense why it turned from where it did. I then get "stuck" with incorrect directional bias, expecting a PAZ to complete, which never does (although sometimes in fairness I've seen an incomplete PAZ move get completed days or even weeks later). I put this down initially to inexperience, but I'm pretty confident in marking up the correct FLs on the charts now, and 9 times out of 10, even if I get something wrong, I understand why with hindsight. It's the 1 in 10 that's driving me nuts. Much of what I see that confuses me can be explained if you consider the DP to engulf a FL/FTR an important level in its own right, but I was under the impression that it was only FTRs and the engulfs thereof that mattered in the scheme of things (assuming of course the DP isn't an important FL/FTR itself). So two questions really - to clarify, is it only the FTR formed after an engulf, and not the DP to engulf that is important? And if so, why do we so often see big turning points take place from engulf DPs, when its corresponding FTR has been taken out? And also, are there just times you have to shrug your shoulders, and move on? Or is it possible to understand every move? All the best, thanks for any help. At the moment I'm thinking I need to readjust my thinking - if we have a pole base which engulfed an important FL, and then a FTR which formed thereafter, I should only consider the "whole thing" engulfed if both the FTR and the pole base are taken out. Hope you don't mind me polluting your great journal.. :) Please consider the following example. This is a USDCAD chart. I watched IF's decision points video over and over again. He says in the video that, price goes from one decision point to another. In the below H1 chart.. I have marked a DP which decides price is too expensive and then decides to go down the SR flip. IF shows that the decision is made at the open of the candle. (at least that's what I saw in the video). 1. But how do we really say that the decision is made at the open of the candle? when we go to LTFs, candle is narrowed down we can exactly find the LTF candle which broke the level. So the DP will be the opening point of the LTF candle.. Still if we have more and more LTFs.. we can further go down and narrow down. So which DP is the correct DP which decides the price is too expensive and broke the S/R flip level? DP1, DP2 or DP3? 2. Other thing is, If price is reacting to a DP, why would we need FTRs, FLs? In the example, the DP is different from the FTR. Price can react to the FTR or DP. Do we put our pending orders at FTR and Stoplosses on DPs ( in this example)? 3. Are FLs, FTRs new decision points made after the previous decision took place? ( For example, DP1 decided that price is too expensive and broke down the SR flip level and then made the new DP2) Note: Price actually reacted to DP1. never reacted to the FTR.. I dont know why. The following user(s) said Thank You: salivan , JAVAD , ericpurdy Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 26 Sep 2016 11:55 #186 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 Hi VT The moment when things clicked for me in my studies was the moment I realized nothing in the market is technical. Instead I started looking for places where I could find unfilled orders. Today, when I look at the chart I am searching for the ultimate bouncing levels, then I consider if I want to trade these levels. At decision points there are unfilled orders and you can expect a bounce there. but be aware that a decision level is just the nearby barrier of a PAZ (price Action Zone). The further boundary of that PAZ can react too, because this is the first level that price was balanced with opposing orders, therefor just beyond it you can find unfilled orders. This is why you should consider FTR, FL, DP, CP, QM, DM - these all are variety of formations that unfold imbalanced order flow that you can use in your trading. To make things more complex, yes you should drill down forever if possible to really fine your level. You should always remember that time is no factor of the market, therefore time frame is just a random necessity to document price. The events of the markets are documented by price only. So our job is harder to extract the finest level to act upon. Yes is no that straight forward, And yes we should be very happy it is like that, only the busy miners get reach the precious rocks. To be more practical, do what I did for weeks during my initial learning phase; take two lines one for above price one for below market price. stare at one minute charts and place you lines where you know price will give a bounce. Do so for whole sessions with great patience and motivation to really see price in action. If you do so, come back and share with the RTM community your finding. I am sure there are going to be at least few priceless insights you take for life. Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 02 Oct 2016 15:46 #192 benhur Topic Author --> Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2225 I got this question in my PM, I think it worth to post my reply in public. The question was related to my definition for Flag-Limits. as some of you know, I tend to breakdown everything to the OF arrays, by doing this I can assume where unfilled orders are located: Think of a Flag-Limit in terms of orderflow balance / imbalance. The flag area show us where there was balanced OF, while the pole (the approach or the break) is where we assume to find imbalance. We also assume that we can find unfilled orders where there was first (or last) imbalanced OF. This, in theory explains the FL under and above. The question was When you said while the pole the approach or the break .. Do you mean the last H made inside a Flag before let's say price Engulfs down with LQ ? and by break you mean the Break of Support or Demand in the Flag ? also can't figure out exactly what do you mean by first or last Imbalanced ? Here is an image to explain how FO aligned around FL Attachments: Check this box to be notified of replies to this topic. Note: BBcode and smileys are still usable. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com Report to moderator Quick Reply Reply Quote Thank You Benhur Journal 12 Oct 2016 15:04 #196 ViTaLDRuG --> Offline Trader Posts: 280 Thank you received: 521 Hi Benhur, I noticed that, if a FL is broken, and then if another FL is created which hold the price up or down (It will hold the price up or down opposite to the direction the previous FL hold) and then the second FL is broken, that breaking swing will always go to the decision point there it was decided to break the first FL. Noticed this almost every time. In case of a perfect Flag (which has a big pole up/down), if its FL is broken, the price should go to the start of the pole. Of course the start of that pole should break another FL on the way to create the current Flag' FL. benhur wrote: ViTaLDRuG wrote: Where do I place these two lines? You mentioned that the levels structure you use most is the QM. Does that mean that you start your analysis by looking for QM levels at significant H4/D1 swing highs or swing lows (which I assume are the extremes)? 2. As for LTF confirmation, are you referring to the LTF structure at the source of the HTF level or LTF entry set-up? Looking at your charts, you based your entry on some LTF entry set-ups when the price returns to the level, but do you also look at the LTF at the source of the HTF level to pinpoint the exact level? Basically I am scanning for QM levels of HTF. If the level itself is at extreme, I would be more comfortable to take it as is. There are more QM levels at Mid way of HTF - those offer scalp opportunities. LTF confirmation would be a newly forming QM structure occurring at logical HTF level and which is also explainable with LTF level at the origin of the HTF level i apply. TF are just HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT price wrapped in arbitrary time period, while price is has a definite targets regardless of TF. Therefore MTF analysis is so much important for our work. IF shows that the decision is made at the open of the candle. (at least that's what I saw in the video). https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 10/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 1. But how do we really say that the decision is made at the open of the candle? when we go to LTFs, candle is narrowed down we can exactly find the LTF candle which broke the level. So the DP will be the opening point of the LTF candle.. Still if we have more and more LTFs.. we can further go down and narrow down. So which DP is the correct DP which decides the price is too expensive and broke the S/R flip level? DP1, DP2 or DP3? 2. Other thing is, If price is reacting to a DP, why would we need FTRs, FLs? Are FLs, FTRs new decision points made after the previous decision took place? ( For example, DP1 decided that price is too expensive and broke down the SR flip level and then made the new DP2) Note: Price actually reacted to DP1. never reacted to the FTR.. I dont know why. Thanks in advance. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 11/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUACttAaTchImOeNnts: FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: salivan, JAVAD, ericpurdy QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Benhur Journal #186 benhur Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2231 Hi VT The moment when things clicked for me in my studies was the moment I realized nothing in the market is technical. Instead I started looking for places where I could find unfilled orders. Today, when I look at the chart I am searching for the ultimate bouncing levels, then I consider if I want to trade these levels. At decision points there are unfilled orders and you can expect a bounce there. but be aware that a decision level is just the nearby barrier of a PAZ (price Action Zone). The further boundary of that PAZ can react too, because this is the first level that price was https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 12/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community balanced with opposing orders, therefor just beyond it you can find unfilled orders. This HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT is why you should consider FTR, FL, DP, CP, QM, DM - these all are variety of formations that unfold imbalanced order flow that you can use in your trading. To make things more complex, yes you should drill down forever if possible to really fine your level. You should always remember that time is no factor of the market, therefore time frame is just a random necessity to document price. The events of the markets are documented by price only. So our job is harder to extract the finest level to act upon. Yes is no that straight forward, And yes we should be very happy it is like that, only the busy miners get reach the precious rocks. To be more practical, do what I did for weeks during my initial learning phase; take two lines one for above price one for below market price. stare at one minute charts and place you lines where you know price will give a bounce. Do so for whole sessions with great patience and motivation to really see price in action. If you do so, come back and share with the RTM community your finding. I am sure there are going to be at least few priceless insights you take for life. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 17/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Benhur Journal #192 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT benhur Offline Master Trader www.the5ers.com Posts: 730 Thank you received: 2231 I got this question in my PM, I think it worth to post my reply in public. The question was related to my definition for Flag-Limits. as some of you know, I tend to breakdown everything to the OF arrays, by doing this I can assume where unfilled orders are located: Think of a Flag-Limit in terms of orderflow balance / imbalance. The flag area show us where there was balanced OF, while the pole (the approach or the break) is where we assume to find imbalance. We also assume that we can find unfilled orders where there was first (or last) imbalanced OF. This, in theory explains the FL under and above. The question was When you said while the pole the approach or the break .. Do you mean the last H made inside a Flag before let's say price Engulfs down with LQ ? and by break you mean the Break of Support or Demand in the Flag ? also can't figure out exactly what do you mean by first or last Imbalanced ? https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 18/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Here is an image to explain how FO aligned around FL HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: hansma, parnelli, philantrop, Rakkain, althoufx, AranJibril, rick86, symon, Nehru82T, Al3cs, omidfxt, salivan, JAVAD, alirezat https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 19/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Never risk your own capital in trading. Risk free account application: www.the5ers.com QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Benhur Journal #196 ViTaLDRuG Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 532 Hi Benhur, I noticed that, if a FL is broken, and then if another FL is created which hold the price up or down (It will hold the price up or down opposite to the direction the previous FL hold) and then the second FL is broken, that breaking swing will always go to the decision point there it was decided to break the first FL. Noticed this almost every time. In case of a perfect Flag (which has a big pole up/down), if its FL is broken, the price should go to the start of the pole. Of course the start of that pole should break another FL on the way to create the current Flag' FL. benhur wrote: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/1564-benhur-journal?start=175 24/30 7/27/2018 Benhur Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community ViTHaLODMRuEG wroteE:DWUhCerAeTdIoOINplace thFeOseRtUwMo lines?
